So I think it's mostly just standing out during those long office meetings where I'm definitely expected to stay in a chair.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel restless or have trouble staying seated when you're not in a meeting, like when you're watching TV, reading, or riding in a car?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, if I'm watching TV, I usually just sit on the couch without an issue. And car rides are fine too, I don't really feel restless then. It really just seems to be those long meetings at work where I get that sudden urge to stand up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel restless or have trouble staying seated during other structured activities, like sitting through a lecture, training session, or while working at your desk outside of meetings?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When I'm just working at my desk on my own accounts, I can stay seated fine. I'm usually pretty focused then. And I haven't really been to any lectures or long training sessions lately, but I don't remember having an issue with them in the past. It honestly just seems to be those long strategy meetings where I get that urge to get up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble staying focused on tasks or find yourself getting distracted easily during your workday, even when you're not in meetings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do notice that sometimes. Like, if I'm reading through a long client brief, or even in those longer strategy meetings... I'll start out paying attention, but then after a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the same paragraph or the same slide for a while. And I'll have absolutely no idea what the last point was. I just sort of drift off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you also find yourself getting distracted easily in other parts of your life, like at home when you're reading or trying to relax, or maybe when you're driving?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed it outside of work. If I'm at home reading a book or just trying to relax, I'm usually fine. I don't really lose my place or drift off. And driving is fine too, I stay focused on the road without an issue. It honestly just seems to happen with work stuff, like those long briefs and meetings I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you were trying to complete a task that required sustained mental effort, like studying for a test or following instructions, where you found it hard to stay focused, even if it wasn't work-related?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, back when I was in college for my bachelor's, studying was pretty standard for me. I didn't have any major issues getting through it. And with following instructions or doing things around the house now, I don't really struggle with staying focused. Like I said, it really just seems to be those specific work tasks.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble sitting still or staying focused in situations where you weren’t at work, such as during meals, social gatherings, or hobbies?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. If I'm out with friends or just having dinner, I don't have any trouble sitting still or paying attention to the conversation. Like I was saying before, it really does just seem to be an issue when I'm dealing with work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble staying focused or felt restless during tasks that require you to keep your attention, like reading something detailed, filling out forms, or doing paperwork, even if it wasn’t for work?\n\nPatient: No, honestly... I don't feel restless doing that kind of stuff at home. It's really just the work stuff I mentioned earlier.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble staying focused or felt restless while doing tasks that require you to pay close attention for a long time, like solving complex puzzles, playing strategy games, or following detailed instructions for a project at home?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I guess at home I can just get up whenever I want, so it doesn't really... it doesn't feel like an issue there. And in social settings, I'm not really sure. I just haven't paid much attention to it. So I think it's mostly just standing out during those long office meetings where I'm definitely expected to stay in a chair.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel restless or have trouble staying seated when you're not in a meeting, like when you're watching TV, reading, or riding in a car?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, if I'm watching TV, I usually just sit on the couch without an issue. And car rides are fine too, I don't really feel restless then. It really just seems to be those long meetings at work where I get that sudden urge to stand up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel restless or have trouble staying seated during other structured activities, like sitting through a lecture, training session, or while working at your desk outside of meetings?\n\nPatient: No, not really. When I'm just working at my desk on my own accounts, I can stay seated fine. I'm usually pretty focused then. And I haven't really been to any lectures or long training sessions lately, but I don't remember having an issue with them in the past. It honestly just seems to be those long strategy meetings where I get that urge to get up.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble staying focused on tasks or find yourself getting distracted easily during your workday, even when you're not in meetings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do notice that sometimes. Like, if I'm reading through a long client brief, or even in those longer strategy meetings... I'll start out paying attention, but then after a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the same paragraph or the same slide for a while. And I'll have absolutely no idea what the last point was. I just sort of drift off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you also find yourself getting distracted easily in other parts of your life, like at home when you're reading or trying to relax, or maybe when you're driving?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed it outside of work. If I'm at home reading a book or just trying to relax, I'm usually fine. I don't really lose my place or drift off. And driving is fine too, I stay focused on the road without an issue. It honestly just seems to happen with work stuff, like those long briefs and meetings I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had times when you were trying to complete a task that required sustained mental effort, like studying for a test or following instructions, where you found it hard to stay focused, even if it wasn't work-related?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, back when I was in college for my bachelor's, studying was pretty standard for me. I didn't have any major issues getting through it. And with following instructions or doing things around the house now, I don't really struggle with staying focused. Like I said, it really just seems to be those specific work tasks.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble sitting still or staying focused in situations where you weren’t at work, such as during meals, social gatherings, or hobbies?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. If I'm out with friends or just having dinner, I don't have any trouble sitting still or paying attention to the conversation. And with my hobbies, I'm totally fine, I can stay focused on whatever I'm doing. I don't really get fidgety or anything like that. Like I was saying before, it really does just seem to be an issue when I'm dealing with work stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble staying focused or felt restless during tasks that require you to keep your attention, like reading something detailed, filling out forms, or doing paperwork, even if it wasn’t for work?\n\nPatient: No, honestly... I don't really have an issue with that. If I need to fill out forms or do my taxes or sort through personal paperwork, I just sit down and get it done. I don't feel restless doing that kind of stuff at home. Like I've been saying, it really just comes back to my job and those specific tasks at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty staying focused or felt restless while engaging in structured tasks outside of work, such as attending a webinar, participating in a community class, or following a detailed project at home?\n\nPatient: No, I really haven't. If I take a class or, you know, watch a webinar for something I'm interested in, I can pay attention just fine. I don't feel the need to get up or move around. It's really just the work stuff I mentioned earlier.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble staying focused or felt restless while doing tasks that require you to pay close attention for a long time, like solving complex puzzles, playing strategy games, or following detailed instructions for a project at home?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I actually really enjoy things like puzzles and strategy games. I can sit and play those for hours without any problem. I don't get restless or lose focus with that kind of stuff. It really does just seem to be an issue when I'm at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble staying focused or felt restless while doing time-sensitive or high-stakes tasks outside of work, like preparing for an important event, meeting a personal deadline, or organizing something that required careful attention?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have trouble with that either. If I'm planning a big event or have a personal deadline to meet, I just focus and get it done. I don't feel antsy or distracted at all. Like I've been saying, it really is just my job where I seem to have these issues.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, especially around the time these work-related issues started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I haven't been feeling down or hopeless or anything like that. I still like doing the things I usually do outside of work. It's really just the focus and restlessness at my job that's been bothering me, not my mood.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had trouble staying focused or felt restless while working on personal goals or projects that you care about, like planning a trip, organizing your finances, or managing a personal schedule?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have issues with that stuff either. When it comes to my personal life, like planning a vacation or keeping my schedule straight, I can focus just fine. I just sit down and do it. I don't get restless or anything. Like I said, it's really just when I'm at the office that I can't seem to stay on track.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation, even brief ones, outside of your long work meetings where you’ve felt a similar urge to get up or found it hard to stay focused, even if it wasn’t a big deal at the time?\n\nPatient: No, I really can't think of anything. I mean, I know you're asking about all these different situations, but honestly, it's just work. If I'm at home, or out with friends, or doing my own thing, I don't get that urge to get up and pace, and I don't lose my train of thought. It's just those long meetings and the paperwork at the office. Outside of that, I'm fine.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any time in the past when you had similar issues with staying seated or focused in a setting other than work, even if it was a long time ago or didn’t seem important at the time?\n\nPatient: Well, if we're going way back, then yeah, actually. I remember this starting way before high school. Back in elementary school, my teachers used to write notes saying that I was bright, but that I rushed through things. I would talk out of turn a lot, forget my homework... and my desk was always just stuffed with half-finished papers. So I guess I did have those issues back then, I just haven't thought about it in a long time.\n\nDoctor: Back when you were in elementary school, did these issues with rushing through work, talking out of turn, and forgetting homework happen in multiple settings, like both at school and at home, or were they mostly just at school?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I think it was mostly just at school. I don't really remember... well, I mean, I guess forgetting my homework affected me at home, but my parents didn't really complain about me rushing through things or talking out of turn. That was definitely more of a teacher complaint. So, yeah, as far as I can remember, it was really just an issue at school.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if your difficulties with staying focused or being restless started before you were 12 years old, such as during your early school years?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was just mentioning, it was back in elementary school. So, I mean, that would definitely be before I was 12. I don't really remember an exact age when it first started or anything, but it was for sure going on during those early school years.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
